# Findings

Every bug or UX miss anyone finds lands here as one row: a Codex or ChatGPT sweep, a stress test, a code review, a failing test, a thing Tyler jots down. A sweep reads this file before it starts, instead of a hand-written "already covered" list, so nothing is checked or argued twice. `apps/desktop/test/findings.test.ts` checks the table.

## Rules
- **Add a row when a finding is verified** (triage-report step 3), before any fix. A new row goes at the end as `F-new-<slug>` (and is mentioned by that name in tests and journeys); `bun run ids` gives it the next free number at merge (`docs/WORKFLOW.md`). Never reuse or renumber a numbered ID.
- **Status** is one of:
  - `open`
  - `fixed #572`
  - `fixed #573`
  - `fixed #570`
  - `fixed #PR` (a new row awaiting a PR number)
  - `not a bug: <why>` (a D-number, or the reason it was ruled out)
  - `Tyler: <the question>` (his call, nothing built)
  - `known limit: <D-number or why>` (accepted as is)
- **Guard** names what keeps a `fixed` row fixed: a phrase from the test or journey that guards it (its title, or the comment that heads a check script), in backticks; the test looks for it in the suite. When no test can reach it (a macOS-only API, pixels, the real Tauri window), write `no test: <why>`. Other statuses leave it `—`.
- **Update the row in the PR that changes it**, so the table is right at every merge.
- A sweep re-reports a row only when it saw the behaviour come back. Then the row goes back to `open`, with the new PR noted in What.

## Running a sweep
A sweep prompt names the surface and the commit under test, points at this file and asks for:
- **Real tasks over single keys.** The diagram sweeps (`2026-11-11-diagram-sweeps/`) found their bugs in study tasks: save, reopen, edit again, be interrupted mid-edit, see a write fail (`window.__ORE_FAIL`, `__ORE_SLOW`). Key-by-key checklists of native text editing mostly pass.
- **Changed surfaces first.** Start from `git log` since the surface's last clean sweep, and from merged features never checked in the app as it runs (WebKit, the release build, the phone).
- **New findings only**, each with steps to reproduce, and a note on any row it saw come back.

| F15 | 2026-11-11 | Mac window | Unchecked in the real Tauri window: whether ⌘Q's 150 ms lets WebKit write drafts, and drafts shared between the quick quiz and the main window. Checked 2026-11-27 in the real window (an "Ore Debug" copy, computer use with the window in front): it did not. ⌘Q never told the pages at all: the standard Quit menu item sends `terminate:`, which this Tauri (tao has no `applicationShouldTerminate`) never turns into ExitRequested, so "quitting" and its 150 ms never ran, and a guess typed under "What do you already know?" just before ⌘Q was gone on the next open. With Quit made Ore's own item the event ran, and 150 ms was still too short for a note. Now Quit exits through the shell, each page writes and answers `quit_ready`, and the app quits on the answers (2 s at most): seen in the copy, 4 to 62 ms. The Dock's Quit and a logout still send `terminate:` and skip it | fixed #576 | `Quit is Ore's own menu item, so the pages hear that the app is quitting and save what he just typed` |

| F736 | 2026-11-25 | iPhone, Reader, source note | (simulated learner run, stopwatch) In his longest book (638 KB) on the phone build in WebKit, typing in the source note after making a highlight lagged on most keys: 51 characters took 7.1 s against 2.1 s before the highlight, 130 ms from a key to the next frame. Emptying the CSS highlight registry brought it back to 1.8 s. It does not happen in Chromium on a 730 KB source (1.7 s before and after), so it is WebKit's. The selection was a scripted Range, not iOS's handles: unconfirmed on a real iPhone. Cause (traced in WebKit on the Mac): any CSS highlight painted in a long source in the page's flow slows each key in the note beside it, a saved highlight after a reload too (6.8 s against 1.8 to 2.2 s); the Mac's layout does not. Fix: on the iPhone, while a field beside the source has the keyboard, the source's highlight tints come off (1.9 to 2.2 s), and are back when he leaves it. The other painted layers (code colours, Find, Listen) could do the same and are not checked | fixed #542 | `while he types in the note beside a source, its highlights' tints are off the page` |

| F761 | 2026-11-27 | Ore Cloud, import | (Ore Cloud security pass) Ore Cloud read every import in the server's own process, so one hostile file or page that ate memory or time (a zip bomb that lies about its sizes still costs 30 s of CPU; a page that never ends) froze or killed the server for everyone signed in, and the import's code could read the server's session secret from its environment. Each import now runs in a process of its own (`isolate.ts`): stopped after 5 minutes with any tool it started, its scratch files removed, first to be killed when memory runs out, and given only the environment the import reads. | F856 | 2026-11-27 | iPhone, e2e | (CI on #583, once, WebKit) the journey "the phone ticks under his finger on four moments" found no Highlight popup after its double tap on a paragraph; the same journey passed on the commit before and 6 of 6 times here. Cause: the journey sent its two taps one after the other, each waiting for the browser to answer, so on a busy runner the second came more than 350 ms after the first, which the Reader rightly reads as two single taps (they show and hide the bars). A 380 ms wait put between the taps gives the same failure every time. Not a bug in the app. `doubleTap` in e2e/harness.ts now sends both taps at once, and the three journeys that double tap a paragraph use it. Reopen if a double tap is still read as two taps: then build the touches in the page, as `swipe` does.  **Back twice after that fix (2026-11-28, on #589 and #590, the page scrolled in the screenshot), so the taps being apart was not the cause on CI.** The cause: the iPhone reader takes no tap on the text until the text is drawn and read in (`ready`; a long text is drawn in slices), and the journey tapped as soon as the first of the text showed. On a slow runner both taps came before that and were dropped; the scroll in the screenshot is the journey bringing its paragraph into view. Shown by holding `ready` back 900 ms: this journey and the Sections one (D316) fail every time, and pass with the fix, as do all of phone-review, phone-reader and phone-lists. `phoneReaderReady` in e2e/harness.ts waits for the reader's marks layer, which is there only once it is ready; `phoneSource` and every journey that opens a source by hand use it. In the app a double tap in a long source's first moment still does nothing, where the Mac keeps an early click and plays it once the text is in (`early`): parity gap, his pick whether it matters. Reopen if a phone journey still acts on the reader too soon.  **Back a third time after #592 (2026-11-28, on #593, run 46112386146).** Its screenshot shows the paragraph in view, no highlight, and the reader's bars hidden. Bars hidden means exactly one single tap was taken: two single taps show them again, two dropped taps leave them, so the first tap was thrown away and the second counted alone. The Reader throws a tap away when a scroll came within 150 ms before it (a tap that stops a scroll is no tap), and the journey had just scrolled its paragraph into view and waited a fixed 300 ms counted in the test, which says nothing of when a busy page sends its scroll. Forced by sending the reader a scroll 290 ms into that wait: the journey fails every time; with the fix it passes. `doubleTap` now waits by the page's own clock until it has drawn and no scroll has come for 250 ms, then sends both taps; the fixed waits before it are gone. Not reproduced: the exact one-tap-taken picture (the forced scroll drops both taps); so a failed journey now prints what the page saw of its last double tap (each scroll and touch, in ms), and a fourth time will name its cause. Reopen with that line. | fixed #594 | `phone: a double tap highlights the paragraph and opens it, a heading and a highlight keep their taps (D224)` |

| F883 | 2026-11-28 | Import, Word (Mac, iPhone, Ore Cloud), tests | (CI's `test` job on #595 twice and once on another branch, traced) A damaged Word file was refused and, beside the refusal, announced as an unhandled rejection (`[xmldom error] element parse error`), printed beside F760's Word test in the full suite (what failed that test was the row for the appearance timer, below). Mammoth's promise library (bluebird) rejects for a part that isn't XML and hands that to the unzipper's built-in promise a moment later; loaded where a page's `MutationObserver` exists (the iPhone app, or after a desktop test left one in the globals) bluebird waits by microtask, checks in that moment and announces it. Alone, it loads without one and waits a whole turn, so it passed. Its announcing is now off for mammoth: every failure of mammoth's reaches the import's own refusal | fixed #599 | `a damaged Word file is refused once, with no second failure left unhandled, also where a page's globals are present` |

| F886 | 2026-11-28 | Tests (iPhone Bold Text test, F760's Word test), `followAppearance` | (master's `test` job red now and then after #599, and on Tyler's Mac in the full suite; traced from CI's timestamps and reproduced in a ten-line test) F760's Word test failed with `error: undefined` about 60 seconds after the iPhone Bold Text test. That test started `followAppearance`, whose once-a-minute question could not be stopped, with a stand-in that answers `Promise.reject()`. The Word test's `expect(…).rejects` waits over a second in Bun 1.4.4 (the refusal settles through `setImmediate` steps), and a timer that fires inside that wait has its rejected promise reported as unhandled before its handler is attached; so the run failed only when the minute landed in that second. `followAppearance` now returns its stop and the test calls it; the Word test awaits its refusal plainly, so it no longer sits in that wait | fixed #601 | `stop leaves no timer or listener behind` |
